You dream. The city sidewalks are uniform—symmetrical in a way that only a dream or an artist could conjure. The air is frigid and you shiver unconsciously, something so human that for a moment you forget your nature. Two figures emerge from the fog ahead of you, and as you get closer, their faces swirl into recognizable form.
*if (ch5_heard_wards_whole_truth)
	Robert Ward and his Banu Haqim associate, Sevinc, are walking together along the sidewalk, heads held closely together as they speak in hushed tones. You walk faster, closing in so you can overhear them.
	
	"—think ${heshe}'s going to come around," Ward is saying. "There has to be an end to this. If any good thing can come of Arundel's disappearance, it's that we'll finally be able to take Corliss down."
	
	Sevinc shakes her head. "You're getting lost in the weeds, Bobbi. You're thinking too much about propping up this crumbling Ivory Tower when you should just let it fall and replace it with something that will serve us all."
	
	Ward responds angrily, and both of their voices rise into a shouting match impossible to comprehend. You grip your ears as pain lances through your body and you're torn away from the Anarchs into a cold, dark void.
	*page_break Through Smoke, A New Vision
	Another place, another time…

	You stare down into your own unblinking eyes and your gaze travels downward over ragged flesh and snapped bone. The air suddenly smells of sulfur. Is this real? Or is your unconscious mind simply trying to make sense of recent events?

*comment An important note here: Alisha is lying--she was never attacked personally. She sent her ghoul, Jonathan, into the sewers to investigate reports that the mortals who attacked her exhibition were hiding down there until the heat from the police died down. Jonathan was killed by a 'Ward against ghouls,' and when she went looking for him and discovered what happened, she was furious, but didn't actually meet any of the Tremere herself. She went to Corliss about it in private. Corliss then had the idea to use the incident as an excuse to rid herself of the Tremere who had helped her remove Arundel (see pitch doc.) As Robert Ward notes in chapter 5, it was inevitable that Corliss would betray the Warlocks or vice-versa. The hidden coterie of Tremere know too much about Corliss's attempted ascension, and she won't want to have it held over her head for the duration of her praxis. If she can use Alisha's rage as an excuse to 'reluctantly' call a hunt on the unwelcome Tremere, it's an excellent cover. Even if the plot fails, Corliss has some plausible deniability when the Tremere come a-knocking. So Corliss conspired with Alisha to include an assault on herself as part of the story, making it easier to excuse the call for a hunt. Alisha gets immediate gratification for the killing of her favorite ghoul (and consort) Jonathan, and Corliss cuts off the inevitable blackmail from the Tremere that would come after her rise to power. (And as a bonus, it gets Kashif off her back.)
